<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> On a smooth connected projective complex variety with smoothly semipositive anticanonical bundle, we prove that asymptotically zero valuation on the curvature-null face is attained by an effective rational anticanonical divisor. The fixed auxiliary line bundle is arbitrary.

- **C01** (theorem, [source](#stmt-v-exact)): With $X,L,\alpha,\nu,G_0,\ell$ as above, let $v$ be either a discrete valuation of $\mathbb C(X)$ centered on $X$ or the zero valuation. Let $M$ be any fixed line bundle. Suppose that effective integral divisors $N_j$ and positive integers $m_j\to\infty$ satisfy $$N_j\sim m_jL+M,\qquad \ell(N_j)=0,\qquad \frac{v(N_j)}{m_j}\longrightarrow0.$$ There is an effective rational divisor $N\sim_{\mathbb Q}L$ such that $v(N)=0$.
  - Explanation: Attains exact zero order in rational anticanonical linear equivalence from a null twisted sequence.
- **C02** (theorem, [source](#stmt-v-invariant-main)): Let $X$ be smooth connected projective over $\mathbb C$, with $-K_X$ smoothly semipositive and $H^i(X,\mathcal O_X)=0$ for every $i>0$. For every algebraic torus $T$ acting on $X$, some positive anticanonical multiple has a nonzero $T$-invariant section for its natural linearization.
  - Explanation: Produces a nonzero section invariant under the torus for the natural anticanonical linearization.
- **C03** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-endpoint)): Let $R\subset K[x,y]$ be a finitely generated $\mathbb C$-subalgebra, graded by $\mathbb N^2$ with $\deg x=(1,0)$, $\deg y=(0,1)$, and $R_{0,0}=\mathbb C$. Let $v$ be a real-valued valuation of $K$, trivial on $\mathbb C^\times$. Suppose that nonzero homogeneous elements $$s_j=f_jx^{a_j}y^{b_j}\in R,\qquad d_j=a_j+b_j>0,$$ satisfy $$\frac{a_j}{d_j}\longrightarrow0, \qquad \frac{v(f_j)}{d_j}\longrightarrow\gamma\in\mathbb R.$$ Then every finite set of nonzero homogeneous generators of $R$ contains a generator $f x^0y^b$, with $b>0$, such that $$\frac{v(f)}b\leq\gamma.$$
  - Explanation: Selects an actual homogeneous generator on the boundary ray with the limiting valuation bound.
- **C04** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-geometric)): Let $X,Y$ be smooth integral projective complex varieties, let $W$ be normal and projective, and let $$X\xleftarrow{\ p\ }W\xrightarrow{\ f\ }Y$$ be morphisms, with $p$ birational and $f$ surjective and equidimensional. Identify $\mathbb C(Y)$ with its induced subfield of $\mathbb C(X)=\mathbb C(W)$. Fix a centered discrete valuation $v$ of $\mathbb C(X)$, allowing also the zero valuation.* *Let $A\geq0$ be a rational divisor on $Y$ such that $p_*f^*A=0$. Let $D_0,D_1$ be rational Cartier divisors on $X$ and put $$D(t)=(1-t)D_0+tD_1\qquad(0\leq t\leq1).$$ For each prime divisor $Q$ of $Y$, define $$\label{eq:minimum-divisor} P(t)=\sum_Q \min_{E:\,f(E)=Q} \frac{\mathop{\mathrm{coeff}}_E p^*D(t)}{\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E(f^*Q)}\,Q,$$ where $E$ ranges over the prime divisors of $W$ dominating $Q$. Assume that $P(t)=(1-t)P(0)+tP(1)$ throughout $[0,1]$.* *Suppose that rational numbers $t_j\in[0,1]$ tend to zero and that $h_j\in\mathbb C(Y)^\times\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb Q$ satisfy $$N_j=D(t_j)+\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_X(h_j)\geq0,\qquad v(N_j)\longrightarrow0.$$ Finally, suppose that for some $\epsilon\in\mathbb Q_{>0}$ and integer $k>0$, the divisors $$B_1=k\bigl(A+\epsilon P(1)\bigr),\qquad B_0=k\bigl(A+\epsilon P(0)\bigr)$$ are Cartier and the ring $R(Y;B_1,B_0)$ is finitely generated. Then there is an effective rational divisor $$N\sim_{\mathbb Q}D_0,\qquad v(N)=0.$$
  - Explanation: Removes an exceptional correction by birational pushforward before using centeredness to obtain exact zero order.
- **C05** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-v-polar-field)): The set $$k=\{0\}\cup\{h\in\mathbb C(X)^\times:\ell((h)_\infty)=0\}$$ is a finitely generated subfield of $\mathbb C(X)$, relatively algebraically closed in $\mathbb C(X)$.
  - Explanation: Identifies the null-polar functions as a finitely generated relatively algebraically closed field.
- **C06** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-v-null-rank)): For every very ample divisor $H$ on $Y$, $$\label{V:ample-null} \ell(\pi_*g^*H)=0.$$ If $E_Z=K_{Z/X}$ is the effective Jacobian divisor, then $$\label{V:zero-rank} \dim_{\mathbb C(Y)}H^0(Z_\eta,\mathcal O_Z(E_Z)\|_{Z_\eta})=1.$$
  - Explanation: Establishes nullity of base polarizations and the full generic adjoint rank needed for metric descent.
- **C07** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cor-arc)): Let $X$ be smooth integral projective over $\mathbb C$, let $L=-K_X$ have a smooth semipositive Hermitian metric, and let $M$ be a line bundle such that $-M$ is pseudoeffective. Let $\gamma$ be a generic formal arc on $X$. Suppose that effective integral divisors satisfy $$N_j\sim M+m_jL,\qquad m_j\in\mathbb Z_{>0},\quad m_j\longrightarrow\infty,\qquad v_\gamma(N_j)=o(m_j).$$ Then, for some integer $r>0$, there is an effective integral divisor $$D\sim rL,\qquad v_\gamma(D)=0.$$
  - Explanation: Clears rational denominators to give an integral anticanonical divisor of zero generic-arc order.
- **C08** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-v-order-weight)): Let an algebraic torus $T$ act on a smooth integral projective variety $X$, and let $L$ be a $T$-linearized line bundle. Let $\lambda:\mathbb G_m\to T$ be a cocharacter, and let $S$ be its source component: a dense open subset of $X$ tends to $S$ as the parameter tends to zero. Let $\mu$ be the $\lambda$-weight on $L\|_S$. If $0\ne s\in H^0(X,L)$ has $\lambda$-weight $w$, then $$v_\lambda(\operatorname{div}s)=\mu-w,$$ where $v_\lambda$ is the order on the arc $t\mapsto\lambda(t)\xi$ and $\xi$ is the generic point, viewed as a $\mathbb C(X)$-valued point. For the zero cocharacter the order and both weights are zero.
  - Explanation: Computes generic-orbit order from the source fiber weight using the declared left action.
- **C09** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-v-convex-product)): Let $T$ act on an integral projective variety $X$, and let $L$ be a $T$-linearized line bundle. Suppose, for $1\le i\le N$, that $s_i\in H^0(X,r_iL)$ are nonzero eigensections with characters $\alpha_i$, where $r_i\in\mathbb Z_{>0}$. If zero lies in the convex hull of the rational points $\alpha_i/r_i$, then $H^0(X,rL)^T\ne0$ for some $r>0$.
  - Explanation: Clears rational convex coefficients and multiplies nonzero eigensections to cancel their characters.
- **C10** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-v-flat-model)): Let $X\dashrightarrow Y$ be a dominant rational map of integral projective varieties over $\mathbb C$. There are a smooth projective birational model $Y'$ of $Y$ and an integral projective flat family $X_0\to Y'$ with a birational morphism to the graph of the original map. Every irreducible component of every fiber has dimension $\dim X-\dim Y$. The normalization $V$ of $X_0$ is equidimensional over $Y'$; in particular, every vertical prime divisor of $V$ maps onto a prime divisor of $Y'$. If $\mathbb C(Y)$ is algebraically closed in $\mathbb C(X)$, the morphism from $V$, and from any smooth resolution of $V$, to $Y'$ has connected fibers. If a torus acts on $X$ and the rational map is invariant, these constructions and a projective resolution of $V$ can be made equivariant, with trivial action on the base.
  - Explanation: Constructs a flat projective model and an equidimensional normalization, retaining equivariance when assumed.
- **C11** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-v-bounded-maximum)): Let $X,Y$ be smooth connected projective varieties, let $L=-K_X$ have a smooth semipositive metric $h$, and consider $$W\xrightarrow{p}X,\qquad W\xrightarrow{f}Y, \qquad Z\xrightarrow{r}W,$$ where $W$ is normal integral projective, $p$ is a birational morphism, $f$ is surjective and equidimensional with connected general fiber, and $r$ is a smooth projective resolution. Write $\pi=pr$, $g=fr$ and $E=K_Z-\pi^*K_X\ge0$. Let $B$ be a line bundle on $Y$, let $a\in\mathbb Z_{>0}$, and suppose $$0\ne\tau\in H^0(W,ap^*L-f^*B)$$ has order zero on at least one component above every prime divisor of $Y$. Assume that, for every integer $l\ge1$, the ordinary generic adjoint space for $K_{Z/Y}+(al+1)\pi^*L$ has dimension one and is generated, in base frames, by $s_E(r^*\tau)^l$. Then $B$ has a semipositive metric with locally bounded weights. On a smooth connected-fiber open set its weight in a frame $\beta$ is the continuous plurisubharmonic function $$\label{V:sup-weight} \varphi_\beta(y)=-\log\max_{Z_y} \|r^*\tau\,g^*\beta\|^2_{(\pi^*h)^a}.$$ In particular, $B$ is nef.* *The same conclusion holds with invariant generic rank one in place of ordinary rank one, provided an algebraic torus acts compatibly on the diagram with trivial action on $Y$, $L$ has its natural linearization, $B$ has trivial linearization, $\tau$ is invariant, and $h$ is invariant under the maximal compact torus.
  - Explanation: Uses every relative adjoint moment to obtain locally bounded semipositive weights and nefness on the base.
- **C12** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-v-nef-descent)): Let $Y$ be smooth connected projective with $q(Y)=0$, and let $B$ be a nef Cartier divisor on $Y$. There are smooth connected projective varieties $Y'$ and $Z$, a birational morphism $\rho:Y'\to Y$, a surjective morphism $h:Y'\to Z$ with connected fibers, and a nef rational Cartier divisor $L$ on $Z$ such that $$\rho^*B\sim_{\mathbb Q}h^*L.$$ If $\dim Z>0$, every irreducible curve through a very general point of $Z$ has positive $L$-degree. After multiplying $B$ and $L$ by one positive integer, $L$ is Cartier and the displayed equivalence is linear.
  - Explanation: Upgrades numerical nef descent to rational linear descent using vanishing irregularity.
- **C13** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-v-sesha)): Let $Z$ be smooth projective of dimension $b>0$, let $L$ be a nef Cartier divisor, and suppose every curve through a very general point of $Z$ has positive $L$-degree. Fix a very ample Cartier divisor $H$ and rational $\epsilon>0$. For every $c>0$, all sufficiently large integers $k$ make $$J_k=\tfrac12(kL+\epsilon H)$$ an ample rational divisor with Seshadri constant greater than $c$ at a very general point. At any such point $z$ one can choose a smooth positive metric of curvature $\eta$ on $J_k$ and a quasi-plurisubharmonic function $\psi$ with neat analytic singularities, smooth away from $z$, such that $$\eta+i\partial\bar\partial\psi\ge0, \qquad \psi(w)\le c\log\|w\|^2+O(1)$$ in coordinates centered at $z$.
  - Explanation: Obtains arbitrarily large Seshadri bounds and isolated logarithmic poles on the reduced base.
- **C14** (theorem, [source](#stmt-v-approximate-arc)): Let $W$ be smooth connected projective over $\mathbb C$, with $q(W)=0$, and let $A=-K_W$ admit a smooth semipositive Hermitian metric. Let $M$ be a line bundle with $-M$ pseudoeffective. Let $v$ be the order along a formal arc over an extension field of $\mathbb C$ whose fraction point maps to the generic point of $W$; the trivial order is allowed. Suppose there are effective integral divisors $$N_i\sim M+m_iA,\qquad 0<m_1<m_2<\cdots, \qquad v(N_i)=O(1).$$ For every real $\delta>0$ there are an integer $r>0$ and an effective integral divisor $N\sim rA$ with $v(N)<\delta r$.
  - Explanation: Transfers bounded twisted arc orders to arbitrarily small normalized anticanonical orders.
- **C15** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-v-extremal-invariants)): Let $W$ be smooth connected projective with $q(W)=0$ and $\chi(W,\mathcal O_W)\ne0$. Suppose $A=-K_W$ has a smooth semipositive Hermitian metric. Let an algebraic torus $T$ act faithfully on $W$, and suppose an equivariantly linearized very ample line bundle is available. For the natural linearization on $A$, there is an integer $r>0$ with $H^0(W,rA)^T\ne0$.
  - Explanation: Uses the Euler characteristic hypothesis and an equivariant very ample line to obtain invariant nonvanishing.
- **C16** (theorem, [source](#stmt-v-invariant-conversion)): Let $X$ be smooth connected projective with $q(X)=0$, and let $A=-K_X$ carry a smooth semipositive Hermitian metric. Let an algebraic torus $T$ act on $X$, and use the natural linearization on $A$. Let $G$ be a pseudoeffective $T$-linearized line bundle. Suppose that for an unbounded set $I$ of positive integers there are nonzero sections $$\delta_j\in H^0(X,jA-G)^T\qquad(j\in I).$$ Then $H^0(X,mA)^T\ne0$ for some integer $m>0$.
  - Explanation: Removes a fixed pseudoeffective error while retaining invariance through the controlled function field.
- **C17** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-v-invariant-differentials)): Let $X,T,A$ satisfy the geometric hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#V:invariant-conversion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="V:invariant-conversion">16</a>. If for a fixed $0\le p\le\dim X$ there are nonzero invariant sections of $\Omega_X^p\otimes jA$ for an unbounded set of positive integers $j$, then $H^0(X,mA)^T\ne0$ for some $m>0$.
  - Explanation: Converts unbounded invariant twisted differential forms into invariant anticanonical nonvanishing.
- **C18** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-v-invariant-index-application)): Under the geometric hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#V:invariant-conversion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="V:invariant-conversion">16</a>, in particular $q(X)=0$, this conversion also supplies the nef-reduction route from invariant cohomology. The invariant-index theorem (OpenAI 2026c, Theorem <span class="upright">(Invariant anticanonical index)</span>) [[CompanionI](#ref-CompanionI)] applies to the compact complex manifold $X$ and its maximal compact torus, with the natural linearization of $A=-K_X$. It says that the invariant index agrees with a rational polynomial on every positive multiple of some integer, and that this polynomial’s value at zero is the actual invariant index at zero. The connected torus acts trivially on ordinary cohomology by homotopy and therefore on its Hodge summands. Consequently this zero value is $\chi(X,\mathcal O_X)$. If it is nonzero, some fixed $q$ has nonzero invariant $H^q(X,mA)$ for unbounded $m$. The compact-averaged hard Lefschetz map with coefficient $(m+1)A$ produces the invariant twisted forms needed by Corollary <a href="#V:invariant-differentials" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="V:invariant-differentials">17</a>. Thus $H^0(X,mA)^T\ne0$ for some $m>0$ whenever $\chi(X,\mathcal O_X)\ne0$. In particular the hypothesis $H^q(X,\mathcal O_X)=0$ for every $q>0$ gives the zero index value $1$ and $q(X)=0$. The index theorem and the conversion play separate roles: the former retains the actual constant term, whereas the latter produces an invariant section without presuming an equivariant version of an ordinary nonvanishing criterion.
  - Explanation: Combines invariant index information with invariant conversion under the stated geometric hypotheses.
- **C19** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-v-controlled-cone)): Let $V$ be smooth, connected, rationally connected and projective over $\mathbb C$. Suppose that $L=-K_V$ has a smooth semipositive metric, and let an algebraic torus $T$ act on $V$. There is a rational polyhedral cone in $\operatorname{Pic}^T(V)_{\mathbb R}$ generated by classes of invariant effective rational divisors and containing $[D]^T$ for every invariant prime $D$ for which $aL-D$ is pseudoeffective for some integer $a>0$.
  - Explanation: Contains controlled invariant prime classes in a rational polyhedral cone of invariant effective classes; the generators need not be controlled.
- **C20** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-v-controlled-rank-metric-data)): For the diagram <a href="#V:controlled-model" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="V:controlled-model">[V:controlled-model]</a> and any very ample divisor $H$ on $Y$, some integer $a>0$ satisfies $$\label{V:controlled-domination} a\pi^*L-f^*H\quad\text{pseudoeffective}.$$ If $E=K_{U/V}$, then $$\label{V:controlled-rank} \operatorname{rank}f_*\mathcal O_U(E)=1.$$ Moreover there is an invariant effective integral divisor $J$ with $$\label{V:controlled-J} (f_*\mathcal O_U(E))^{**}=\mathcal O_Y(J), \qquad p^\diamond[J]^T=0,$$ where the first identification uses the canonical inclusion of constants.
  - Explanation: Establishes base domination, generic adjoint rank, and an invariant correction that disappears under rational pullback.
- **C21** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-v-bounded-above-weight)): Let $V$ be smooth connected projective with $H^i(V,\mathcal O_V)=0$ for $i>0$, and let $v:\mathbb G_m\to\operatorname{Aut}(V)$ be an algebraic action. Give $L=-K_V$ its natural linearization. There is a constant $C$ such that, for every sufficiently large integer $m$, some $H^q(V,mL)$ has a $v$-weight at most $C$.
  - Explanation: Bounds some actual cohomology weight uniformly above as the positive twist grows.
- **C22** (theorem, [source](#stmt-v-controlled-invariant-nonvanishing)): Let $V$ be a smooth connected rationally connected projective complex variety with smoothly semipositive $-K_V$. For every algebraic torus $T$ acting on $V$, there is an integer $m>0$ such that $$H^0(V,-mK_V)^T\ne0$$ with the natural anticanonical linearization.
  - Explanation: Obtains invariant anticanonical nonvanishing in the rationally connected case through the controlled cone.

      "exact_statement": "Let $X,Y$ be smooth integral projective complex varieties, let $W$ be normal and projective, and let $$X\\xleftarrow{\\ p\\ }W\\xrightarrow{\\ f\\ }Y$$ be morphisms, with $p$ birational and $f$ surjective and equidimensional. Identify $\\mathbb C(Y)$ with its induced subfield of $\\mathbb C(X)=\\mathbb C(W)$. Fix a centered discrete valuation $v$ of $\\mathbb C(X)$, allowing also the zero valuation.*\n\n\n*Let $A\\geq0$ be a rational divisor on $Y$ such that $p_*f^*A=0$. Let $D_0,D_1$ be rational Cartier divisors on $X$ and put $$D(t)=(1-t)D_0+tD_1\\qquad(0\\leq t\\leq1).$$ For each prime divisor $Q$ of $Y$, define $$\\label{eq:minimum-divisor}\nP(t)=\\sum_Q\n\\min_{E:\\,f(E)=Q}\n\\frac{\\mathop{\\mathrm{coeff}}_E p^*D(t)}{\\mathop{\\mathrm{ord}}_E(f^*Q)}\\,Q,$$ where $E$ ranges over the prime divisors of $W$ dominating $Q$. Assume that $P(t)=(1-t)P(0)+tP(1)$ throughout $[0,1]$.*\n\n*Suppose that rational numbers $t_j\\in[0,1]$ tend to zero and that $h_j\\in\\mathbb C(Y)^\\times\\otimes_{\\mathbb Z}\\mathbb Q$ satisfy $$N_j=D(t_j)+\\mathop{\\mathrm{div}}_X(h_j)\\geq0,\\qquad v(N_j)\\longrightarrow0.$$ Finally, suppose that for some $\\epsilon\\in\\mathbb Q_{>0}$ and integer $k>0$, the divisors $$B_1=k\\bigl(A+\\epsilon P(1)\\bigr),\\qquad\nB_0=k\\bigl(A+\\epsilon P(0)\\bigr)$$ are Cartier and the ring $R(Y;B_1,B_0)$ is finitely generated. Then there is an effective rational divisor $$N\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}D_0,\\qquad v(N)=0.$$",
      "explanation": "Removes an exceptional correction by birational pushforward before using centeredness to obtain exact zero order.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-v-null-rank",
        "exact_quote": "For every very ample divisor $H$ on $Y$, $$\\label{V:ample-null}\n \\ell(\\pi_*g^*H)=0.$$ If $E_Z=K_{Z/X}$ is the effective Jacobian divisor, then $$\\label{V:zero-rank}\n \\dim_{\\mathbb C(Y)}H^0(Z_\\eta,\\mathcal O_Z(E_Z)|_{Z_\\eta})=1.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-068.7"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-068.7"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-arc",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be smooth integral projective over $\\mathbb C$, let $L=-K_X$ have a smooth semipositive Hermitian metric, and let $M$ be a line bundle such that $-M$ is pseudoeffective. Let $\\gamma$ be a generic formal arc on $X$. Suppose that effective integral divisors satisfy $$N_j\\sim M+m_jL,\\qquad\nm_j\\in\\mathbb Z_{>0},\\quad m_j\\longrightarrow\\infty,\\qquad\nv_\\gamma(N_j)=o(m_j).$$ Then, for some integer $r>0$, there is an effective integral divisor $$D\\sim rL,\\qquad v_\\gamma(D)=0.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-068.7"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-068.7"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-v-order-weight",
        "exact_quote": "Let an algebraic torus $T$ act on a smooth integral projective variety $X$, and let $L$ be a $T$-linearized line bundle. Let $\\lambda:\\mathbb G_m\\to T$ be a cocharacter, and let $S$ be its source component: a dense open subset of $X$ tends to $S$ as the parameter tends to zero. Let $\\mu$ be the $\\lambda$-weight on $L|_S$. If $0\\ne s\\in H^0(X,L)$ has $\\lambda$-weight $w$, then $$v_\\lambda(\\operatorname{div}s)=\\mu-w,$$ where $v_\\lambda$ is the order on the arc $t\\mapsto\\lambda(t)\\xi$ and $\\xi$ is the generic point, viewed as a $\\mathbb C(X)$-valued point. For the zero cocharacter the order and both weights are zero.",
        "source_id": "PM-068.7"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $T$ act on an integral projective variety $X$, and let $L$ be a $T$-linearized line bundle.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-v-flat-model",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X\\dashrightarrow Y$ be a dominant rational map of integral projective varieties over $\\mathbb C$. There are a smooth projective birational model $Y'$ of $Y$ and an integral projective flat family $X_0\\to Y'$ with a birational morphism to the graph of the original map. Every irreducible component of every fiber has dimension $\\dim X-\\dim Y$. The normalization $V$ of $X_0$ is equidimensional over $Y'$; in particular, every vertical prime divisor of $V$ maps onto a prime divisor of $Y'$. If $\\mathbb C(Y)$ is algebraically closed in $\\mathbb C(X)$, the morphism from $V$, and from any smooth resolution of $V$, to $Y'$ has connected fibers. If a torus acts on $X$ and the rational map is invariant, these constructions and a projective resolution of $V$ can be made equivariant, with trivial action on the base.",
        "source_id": "PM-068.7"
      },

If a torus acts on $X$ and the rational map is invariant, these constructions and a projective resolution of $V$ can be made equivariant, with trivial action on the base.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-v-bounded-maximum",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X,Y$ be smooth connected projective varieties, let $L=-K_X$ have a smooth semipositive metric $h$, and consider $$W\\xrightarrow{p}X,\\qquad W\\xrightarrow{f}Y,\n \\qquad Z\\xrightarrow{r}W,$$ where $W$ is normal integral projective, $p$ is a birational morphism, $f$ is surjective and equidimensional with connected general fiber, and $r$ is a smooth projective resolution. Write $\\pi=pr$, $g=fr$ and $E=K_Z-\\pi^*K_X\\ge0$. Let $B$ be a line bundle on $Y$, let $a\\in\\mathbb Z_{>0}$, and suppose $$0\\ne\\tau\\in H^0(W,ap^*L-f^*B)$$ has order zero on at least one component above every prime divisor of $Y$. Assume that, for every integer $l\\ge1$, the ordinary generic adjoint space for $K_{Z/Y}+(al+1)\\pi^*L$ has dimension one and is generated, in base frames, by $s_E(r^*\\tau)^l$. Then $B$ has a semipositive metric with locally bounded weights. On a smooth connected-fiber open set its weight in a frame $\\beta$ is the continuous plurisubharmonic function $$\\label{V:sup-weight}\n \\varphi_\\beta(y)=-\\log\\max_{Z_y}\n       |r^*\\tau\\,g^*\\beta|^2_{(\\pi^*h)^a}.$$ In particular, $B$ is nef.*\n\n*The same conclusion holds with invariant generic rank one in place of ordinary rank one, provided an algebraic torus acts compatibly on the diagram with trivial action on $Y$, $L$ has its natural linearization, $B$ has trivial linearization, $\\tau$ is invariant, and $h$ is invariant under the maximal compact torus.",
        "source_id": "PM-068.7"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-v-nef-descent",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be smooth connected projective with $q(Y)=0$, and let $B$ be a nef Cartier divisor on $Y$. There are smooth connected projective varieties $Y'$ and $Z$, a birational morphism $\\rho:Y'\\to Y$, a surjective morphism $h:Y'\\to Z$ with connected fibers, and a nef rational Cartier divisor $L$ on $Z$ such that $$\\rho^*B\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}h^*L.$$ If $\\dim Z>0$, every irreducible curve through a very general point of $Z$ has positive $L$-degree. After multiplying $B$ and $L$ by one positive integer, $L$ is Cartier and the displayed equivalence is linear.",
        "source_id": "PM-068.7"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-v-nef-descent",

After multiplying $B$ and $L$ by one positive integer, $L$ is Cartier and the displayed equivalence is linear.",
          "source_id": "PM-068.7"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-v-sesha",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be smooth projective of dimension $b>0$, let $L$ be a nef Cartier divisor, and suppose every curve through a very general point of $Z$ has positive $L$-degree. Fix a very ample Cartier divisor $H$ and rational $\\epsilon>0$. For every $c>0$, all sufficiently large integers $k$ make $$J_k=\\tfrac12(kL+\\epsilon H)$$ an ample rational divisor with Seshadri constant greater than $c$ at a very general point. At any such point $z$ one can choose a smooth positive metric of curvature $\\eta$ on $J_k$ and a quasi-plurisubharmonic function $\\psi$ with neat analytic singularities, smooth away from $z$, such that $$\\eta+i\\partial\\bar\\partial\\psi\\ge0,\n \\qquad \\psi(w)\\le c\\log|w|^2+O(1)$$ in coordinates centered at $z$.",
        "source_id": "PM-068.7"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-v-sesha",

          "source_id": "PM-068.7"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-v-approximate-arc",
        "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be smooth connected projective over $\\mathbb C$, with $q(W)=0$, and let $A=-K_W$ admit a smooth semipositive Hermitian metric. Let $M$ be a line bundle with $-M$ pseudoeffective. Let $v$ be the order along a formal arc over an extension field of $\\mathbb C$ whose fraction point maps to the generic point of $W$; the trivial order is allowed. Suppose there are effective integral divisors $$N_i\\sim M+m_iA,\\qquad 0<m_1<m_2<\\cdots,\n \\qquad v(N_i)=O(1).$$ For every real $\\delta>0$ there are an integer $r>0$ and an effective integral divisor $N\\sim rA$ with $v(N)<\\delta r$.",
        "source_id": "PM-068.7"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-v-approximate-arc",

        "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be smooth connected projective with $q(W)=0$ and $\\chi(W,\\mathcal O_W)\\ne0$. Suppose $A=-K_W$ has a smooth semipositive Hermitian metric.         "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be smooth connected projective with $q(X)=0$, and let $A=-K_X$ carry a smooth semipositive Hermitian metric. Suppose that for an unbounded set $I$ of positive integers there are nonzero sections $$\\delta_j\\in H^0(X,jA-G)^T\\qquad(j\\in I).$$ Then $H^0(X,mA)^T\\ne0$ for some integer $m>0$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-v-invariant-index-application",
        "exact_quote": "Under the geometric hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#V:invariant-conversion\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"V:invariant-conversion\">16</a>, in particular $q(X)=0$, this conversion also supplies the nef-reduction route from invariant cohomology. The invariant-index theorem (OpenAI 2026c, Theorem <span class=\"upright\">(Invariant anticanonical index)</span>) [[CompanionI](#ref-CompanionI)] applies to the compact complex manifold $X$ and its maximal compact torus, with the natural linearization of $A=-K_X$. It says that the invariant index agrees with a rational polynomial on every positive multiple of some integer, and that this polynomial’s value at zero is the actual invariant index at zero. The connected torus acts trivially on ordinary cohomology by homotopy and therefore on its Hodge summands. Consequently this zero value is $\\chi(X,\\mathcal O_X)$. If it is nonzero, some fixed $q$ has nonzero invariant $H^q(X,mA)$ for unbounded $m$. The compact-averaged hard Lefschetz map with coefficient $(m+1)A$ produces the invariant twisted forms needed by Corollary <a href=\"#V:invariant-differentials\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"V:invariant-differentials\">17</a>. Thus $H^0(X,mA)^T\\ne0$ for some $m>0$ whenever $\\chi(X,\\mathcal O_X)\\ne0$. In particular the hypothesis $H^q(X,\\mathcal O_X)=0$ for every $q>0$ gives the zero index value $1$ and $q(X)=0$. The index theorem and the conversion play separate roles: the former retains the actual constant term, whereas the latter produces an invariant section without presuming an equivariant version of an ordinary nonvanishing criterion.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **null functional** ([source](#V:null-functional)): Define $$\label{V:null-functional} \ell(D)= \begin{cases} D\cdot L^\nu\cdot G_0^{n-\nu-1},&\nu<n,\\ 0,&\nu=n. \end{cases}$$
- **centered discrete valuation** ([source](#V:null-functional)): A discrete valuation $v$ of $\mathbb C(X)$ is *centered on $X$* if its valuation ring dominates the local ring of a point of $X$.
- **generic formal arc** ([source](#sec:arc)): A generic formal arc on $X$ is a morphism $$\gamma:\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}F[[t]]\longrightarrow X$$ over an extension field $F/\mathbb C$, whose fraction point maps to the generic point of $X$.
- **bigraded section ring** ([source](#sec:geometric)): For two Cartier divisors $B_1,B_0$, their bigraded section ring is $$\label{eq:section-ring} R(Y;B_1,B_0) =\bigoplus_{a,b\in\mathbb N} H^0\bigl(Y,\mathcal O_Y(aB_1+bB_0)\bigr)x^ay^b \subset\mathbb C(Y)[x,y].$$
- **controlled prime divisor** ([source](#V:controlled-cone-section)): Call a prime divisor $D$ on $V$ *controlled* if $aL-D$ is pseudoeffective for some integer $a>0$.
- **multiplier ideal** ([source](#pm-h-21)): If a singular Hermitian metric has squared frame norm $h=e^{-\varphi}$, its multiplier ideal $\mathcal I(h)$, also denoted $\mathcal J(h)$, consists of holomorphic germs $u$ for which $\|u\|^2e^{-\varphi}$ is locally integrable.
- **neat analytic singularities** ([source](#pm-h-21)): A quasi-plurisubharmonic function has *neat analytic singularities* if locally it has the form $c\log\sum_j\|f_j\|^2+u$, where $c>0$, the $f_j$ are holomorphic and $u$ is smooth.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R01** [claim](#stmt-lem-endpoint) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-endpoint): Finite homogeneous generators and the valuation inequality. -> A boundary generator meeting the limiting valuation bound.
- **R02** [claim](#stmt-v-polar-field) / [proof](#proof-stmt-v-polar-field): Pole inequalities and relative algebraic closure. -> A finitely generated null-polar field.
- **R03** [claim](#stmt-v-null-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-v-null-rank): Null polar bounds and generic-fiber division by the Jacobian section. -> Full generic adjoint rank and null base polarizations.
- **R04** [claim](#stmt-prop-geometric) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-geometric): Endpoint generator selection followed by exceptional pushforward. -> An effective endpoint divisor of exact zero valuation.
- **R05** [claim](#stmt-v-exact) / [proof](#V:exact-proof): Toroidal volume metric and a correction section ring. -> Exact attainment in rational anticanonical linear equivalence.
- **R06** [claim](#stmt-v-invariant-main) / [proof](#pm-h-17): Exact source weights and rational convex multiplication. -> A nonzero invariant anticanonical section.
- **R07** [claim](#stmt-v-invariant-conversion) / [proof](#pm-h-36): Invariant nef descent and multiplier-ideal quotient extension. -> Invariant anticanonical nonvanishing.
- **R08** [claim](#stmt-v-controlled-invariant-nonvanishing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-v-controlled-invariant-nonvanishing): Controlled cone and character separation. -> Invariant anticanonical nonvanishing through the controlled cone.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I1**: Null intersection and asymptotically zero normalized order produce an effective rational anticanonical representative of exactly zero order.
- **I2**: A nonzero invariant section exists in some positive anticanonical multiple for the natural linearization.
- **I3**: Clearing denominators of a rational convex balance of normalized characters gives a nonzero invariant product of the supplied eigensections.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A1**: Treat an asymptotic normalized zero order as a section nonvanishing at the center. -> Every input divisor may still vanish at the same center.
- **A2**: Construct sections from numerical effective classes alone. -> Numerical limits lose the rational functions and rational linear equivalence needed for sections.
- **A3**: Apply torus-invariant nonvanishing with an arbitrary linearization. -> An unspecified character shift changes which sections are invariant.
- **A4**: Use a balanced character list as a procedure for finding geometric eigensections. -> The finite character operation supplies product exponents but does not supply the factors.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The character-product lemma has a finite denominator-clearing step when the nonzero eigensections and a rational convex balance are supplied. The geometric existence results are not computed.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON batch of integral characters alpha, positive degrees r, rational coefficients c=[numerator,denominator].
# PRECONDITIONS: nonempty lists; common character dimension; c nonnegative, sums to one, balances alpha/r exactly.
# OUTPUTS: nonnegative integer exponents n, not all zero, and positive total degree R=sum(n_i*r_i).
# TIME: O(N*d+N) exact arithmetic operations per case; integer bit cost depends on input and common-denominator sizes.
# SPACE: O(N+d) exact numbers per case, excluding input and output batch storage.
# ARITHMETIC: certified exact FLINT fmpq and fmpz; no floating point.
# FAILURE: malformed or unbalanced input raises ValueError and exits nonzero; no geometric section is constructed.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpq, fmpz

def construct(data):
    alpha, degrees, pairs = data['alpha'], data['r'], data['c']
    size = len(degrees)
    if not size or len(alpha) != size or len(pairs) != size:
        raise ValueError('inconsistent lengths')
    dim = len(alpha[0])
    if any(len(row) != dim or any(type(z) is not int for z in row) for row in alpha):
        raise ValueError('characters must be integral vectors')
    if any(type(t) is not int or t <= 0 for t in degrees):
        raise ValueError('positive degrees required')
    if any(len(pair) != 2 or any(type(t) is not int for t in pair) or pair[1] <= 0 for pair in pairs):
        raise ValueError('rational coefficient format')
    coeff = [fmpq(a, b) for a, b in pairs]
    if any(t < 0 for t in coeff) or sum(coeff, fmpq(0)) != 1:
        raise ValueError('not convex coefficients')
    q = [coeff[i] / degrees[i] for i in range(size)]
    if any(sum((q[i] * alpha[i][j] for i in range(size)), fmpq(0)) != 0 for j in range(dim)):
        raise ValueError('characters do not balance')
    scale = fmpz(1)
    for t in q:
        scale = scale.lcm(t.denominator)
    exponents = [int(t * scale) for t in q]
    total = sum(exponents[i] * degrees[i] for i in range(size))
    return {'n': exponents, 'R': total}

batch = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())
print(json.dumps([construct(case) for case in batch]))
```

    "oracle_code": "import json\nimport sys\nfrom fractions import Fraction\n\ninstances = json.loads(open(sys.argv[1]).read())\nanswers = json.loads(open(sys.argv[2]).read())\nassert sys.argv[3] == 'VALID', 'wrong expected marker'\nassert isinstance(answers, list) and len(answers) == len(instances)\nfor item, answer in zip(instances, answers):\n    powers = answer['n']\n    degree = answer['R']\n    weights = item['alpha']\n    twists = item['r']\n    barycentric = [Fraction(*pair) for pair in item['c']]\n    assert len(powers) == len(twists)\n    assert all(type(power) is int and power >= 0 for power in powers)\n    assert any(powers)\n    assert type(degree) is int and degree > 0\n    assert degree == sum(power * twist for power, twist in zip(powers, twists))\n    assert sum(barycentric) == 1 and all(value >= 0 for value in barycentric)\n    for coordinate in zip(*weights):\n        assert sum(power * character for power, character in zip(powers, coordinate)) == 0\n        assert sum(value * Fraction(character, twist) for value, character, twist in zip(barycentric, coordinate, twists)) == 0\n    for power, twist, value in zip(powers, twists, barycentric):\n        assert Fraction(power * twist, degree) == value\n",

    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON batch of integral characters alpha, positive degrees r, rational coefficients c=[numerator,denominator].\n# PRECONDITIONS: nonempty lists; common character dimension; c nonnegative, sums to one, balances alpha/r exactly.\n# OUTPUTS: nonnegative integer exponents n, not all zero, and positive total degree R=sum(n_i*r_i).\n# TIME: O(N*d+N) exact arithmetic operations per case; integer bit cost depends on input and common-denominator sizes.\n# SPACE: O(N+d) exact numbers per case, excluding input and output batch storage.\n# ARITHMETIC: certified exact FLINT fmpq and fmpz; no floating point.\n# FAILURE: malformed or unbalanced input raises ValueError and exits nonzero; no geometric section is constructed.\nimport json, sys\nfrom flint import fmpq, fmpz\n\ndef construct(data):\n    alpha, degrees, pairs = data['alpha'], data['r'], data['c']\n    size = len(degrees)\n    if not size or len(alpha) != size or len(pairs) != size:\n        raise ValueError('inconsistent lengths')\n    dim = len(alpha[0])\n    if any(len(row) != dim or any(type(z) is not int for z in row) for row in alpha):\n        raise ValueError('characters must be integral vectors')\n    if any(type(t) is not int or t <= 0 for t in degrees):\n        raise ValueError('positive degrees required')\n    if any(len(pair) != 2 or any(type(t) is not int for t in pair) or pair[1] <= 0 for pair in pairs):\n        raise ValueError('rational coefficient format')\n    coeff = [fmpq(a, b) for a, b in pairs]\n    if any(t < 0 for t in coeff) or sum(coeff, fmpq(0)) != 1:\n        raise ValueError('not convex coefficients')\n    q = [coeff[i] / degrees[i] for i in range(size)]\n    if any(sum((q[i] * alpha[i][j] for i in range(size)), fmpq(0)) != 0 for j in range(dim)):\n        raise ValueError('characters do not balance')\n    scale = fmpz(1)\n    for t in q:\n        scale = scale.lcm(t.denominator)\n    exponents = [int(t * scale) for t in q]\n    total = sum(exponents[i] * degrees[i] for i in range(size))\n    return {'n': exponents, 'R': total}\n\nbatch = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())\nprint(json.dumps([construct(case) for case in batch]))\n",

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L01** (hypothesis): Exact attainment requires smooth connected projectivity over the complex field, smooth semipositivity, null intersection, and the stated asymptotic sequence. Blocked use: ['Replacing smooth semipositivity by nefness alone.']
- **L02** (hypothesis): The valuation in exact attainment must be a centered discrete valuation or the zero valuation. Blocked use: ['Treating an arbitrary engineering score or an uncentered valuation as the prescribed order.']
- **L03** (hypothesis): The auxiliary line bundle in exact attainment is arbitrary and fixed; the formal-arc corollary separately imposes pseudoeffectivity of its negative. Blocked use: ['Imposing the corollary restriction on the exact theorem, or removing it from the corollary.']
- **L04** (approximation): Asymptotically zero normalized order does not itself supply a section nonvanishing at the center. Blocked use: ['Using a small normalized order as an exact nonvanishing certificate.']
- **L05** (formal_scope): Rational linear equivalence and actual section coefficients are essential to the attainment argument. Blocked use: ['Replacing the section ring by a limiting cone of numerical classes.']
- **L06** (hypothesis): The boundary-generator argument requires finite generation and a real-valued valuation trivial on the complex scalars. Blocked use: ['Applying the endpoint assertion to an arbitrary infinitely generated graded algebra.']
- **L07** (edge_case): The birational correction is removed by pushforward before centeredness gives exact zero order; it need not have zero valuation itself. Blocked use: ['Discarding a correction merely because it is exceptional on a higher model.']
- **L08** (hypothesis): The geometric bridge requires an equidimensional surjective morphism and affine minimum divisors on the selected segment. Blocked use: ['Applying the bridge to a model with vertical divisors over higher codimension without proving its stated hypotheses.']
- **L09** (hypothesis): Invariant anticanonical nonvanishing uses the natural linearization and the stated higher structure-sheaf cohomology vanishing. Blocked use: ['Replacing the natural linearization by an unspecified character twist.']
- **L10** (formal_scope): The orbit order equals the source fiber weight minus the section weight under the declared left action. Blocked use: ['Reversing the weight sign without changing the action convention.']
- **L11** (nonconstructive): The finite character-product step assumes that nonzero eigensections are already supplied. Character balancing does not find those sections. Blocked use: ['Promoting denominator clearing to an algorithm for geometric anticanonical section existence.']
- **L12** (hypothesis): The bounded maximum metric requires generic adjoint rank one for every relative twist; its invariant version has separate equivariance assumptions. Blocked use: ['Replacing all relative rank hypotheses by eventual rank one or substituting invariant rank for ordinary rank without the extra assumptions.']
- **L13** (formal_scope): The maximum metric has locally bounded weights globally; continuity is asserted on the smooth locus. Blocked use: ['Claiming smoothness or global continuity of the descended metric.']
- **L14** (hypothesis): The numerical-to-rational-linear descent of a nef divisor assumes vanishing irregularity. Blocked use: ['Turning numerical pullback into rational linear pullback without the irregularity hypothesis.']
- **L15** (approximation): The independent approximate arc transfer assumes vanishing irregularity, a pseudoeffective negative auxiliary bundle, and bounded unnormalized input orders. Blocked use: ['Dropping those hypotheses merely because the exact theorem has a different regime.']
- **L16** (formal_scope): The controlled metric input is weighted local integrability of the canonical correction section; unweighted integrability is not required here. Blocked use: ['Replacing the weighted input by a claimed unweighted integrability conclusion.']
- **L17** (formal_scope): The finite cone contains controlled invariant prime classes, but its effective generators need not be controlled. Blocked use: ['Claiming that finite generation makes every cone generator controlled.']
- **L18** (hypothesis): The monodromy fixed-vector step uses a connected commutative linear algebraic group and retains its unipotent part. Blocked use: ['Treating a torus-fixed section as monodromy-fixed for an arbitrary algebraic group.']
- **L19** (formal_scope): The product description for compact monodromy is on the universal cover; the finite cover need not split as a product. Blocked use: ['Claiming a product decomposition of a finite cover from the universal-cover description.']
- **L20** (unknown): ABSENT: a general finite procedure and a computational complexity bound for constructing the geometric sections are not supplied. Blocked use: ['Assigning a section-search runtime from geometric finite generation alone.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


On a smooth connected projective complex variety with smoothly semipositive anticanonical bundle, we prove that asymptotically zero valuation on the curvature-null face is attained by an effective rational anticanonical divisor. The fixed auxiliary line bundle is arbitrary.

<a id="V:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

An effective divisor can have normalized order tending to zero while vanishing at the same center in every degree. For example, order one becomes negligible after division by an unbounded sequence of integers. Producing a section which is actually nonzero at that center is a stronger problem. We prove an exact attainment theorem for divisors approaching the anticanonical class of a smooth projective variety with a smoothly semipositive anticanonical bundle. We then use generic orbit arcs to translate this control of orders into statements about characters and invariant sections.

The broader nonvanishing problem asks whether a nef anticanonical bundle on a smooth projective variety has a nonzero section in some positive power. Numerical effectivity and effectivity in rational linear equivalence are distinct here. Lazić, Matsumura, Peternell, Tsakanikas and Xie prove numerical effectivity for smooth projective threefolds with nef anticanonical class and develop nonvanishing methods based on twisted differential forms (Lazić et al. 2023, Theorem A and Section 5) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[LMPTX2023](#ref-LMPTX2023)]. Müller proves anti-log-canonical nonvanishing for projective klt threefold pairs with nef anti-log-canonical divisor (Müller 2025, Corollary B) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Muller2025](#ref-Muller2025)]. Our positivity hypothesis is smooth semipositivity, and our first result retains both rational linear equivalence and the order at a prescribed valuation.

<a id="V:null-functional"></a>
Let $X$ be a smooth connected projective complex variety, of dimension $n>0$, and put $L=-K_X$. Fix a smooth Hermitian metric on $L$ with semipositive curvature form $\alpha$, normalized to represent $c_1(L)$. Let $\nu$ be the maximum complex rank of $\alpha$, and let $G_0$ be an ample line bundle. Define $$\label{V:null-functional}
 \ell(D)=
 \begin{cases}
 D\cdot L^\nu\cdot G_0^{n-\nu-1},&\nu<n,\\
 0,&\nu=n.
 \end{cases}$$ This functional is nonnegative on effective divisors. Moreover $\ell(L)=0$, since $\alpha^{\nu+1}=0$ when $\nu<n$. A discrete valuation $v$ of $\mathbb C(X)$ is *centered on $X$* if its valuation ring dominates the local ring of a point of $X$. We allow the zero valuation as well. The value $v(D)$ of a Cartier divisor is the valuation of a local defining rational function at the center. It is well defined because units have value zero, and extends rationally to rational divisors.

<div class="samepage">

<a id="stmt-v-exact"></a>
<div id="V:exact" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Exact attainment of zero order). *With $X,L,\alpha,\nu,G_0,\ell$ as above, let $v$ be either a discrete valuation of $\mathbb C(X)$ centered on $X$ or the zero valuation. Let $M$ be any fixed line bundle. Suppose that effective integral divisors $N_j$ and positive integers $m_j\to\infty$ satisfy $$N_j\sim m_jL+M,\qquad \ell(N_j)=0,\qquad
 \frac{v(N_j)}{m_j}\longrightarrow0.$$ There is an effective rational divisor $N\sim_{\mathbb Q}L$ such that $v(N)=0$.*

</div>

</div>

The null intersection condition is the geometric input; the auxiliary line $M$ is arbitrary. No irregularity or Euler-characteristic hypothesis is used. The theorem preserves rational linear equivalence, which is necessary for constructing sections. A limit in the numerical effective cone would not give its conclusion.

Here is a useful special case. A *generic formal arc* on $X$ is a morphism $\gamma:\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}F[[t]]\to X$, for an extension field $F/\mathbb C$, whose fraction point maps to the generic point of $X$. If $-M$ is pseudoeffective and $N_j\sim m_jL+M$ is effective, nef intersection gives $0\le\ell(N_j)=\ell(M)\le0$. Thus, if the orders of $N_j$ along $\gamma$ are $o(m_j)$, Theorem <a href="#V:exact" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="V:exact">1</a> supplies a section of some positive multiple of $L$ whose order along $\gamma$ is exactly zero. Corollary <a href="#cor:arc" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:arc">7</a> verifies the centered-valuation and integrality details, including a point variety and the zero valuation.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Why finite generation attains the limit

The proof first places the principal corrections to the given divisors in one function field, and then uses finite generation to attain the limiting class. Let $k\subset\mathbb C(X)$ be the field of rational functions whose polar divisors have $\ell$-value zero. It is relatively algebraically closed. Two fixed input divisors give representatives of $L+M/m_j$ on a common finite support, and the principal corrections between those representatives and $N_j/m_j$ belong to $k$.

The geometric task is to find a model $Y$ of $k$ on which these corrections lie in a finitely generated divisor section ring. On a smooth resolution $\pi:Z\to X$ of the rational map to $Y$, the null-polar field makes the section space of the effective divisor $K_{Z/X}$ one-dimensional on the generic fiber over $Y$. The null intersection also forces the curvature kernel to be vertical on a nonempty open set. Section <a href="#V:null-field" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="V:null-field">3</a> verifies these two inputs to the volume-metric theorem of *Metric descent and rank-preserving contractions* (OpenAI 2026d, Theorem <span class="upright">(Toroidal volume metric)</span>) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[CompanionG](#ref-CompanionG)]. It gives an effective rational correction $A_0$ on $Y$ whose pullback is exceptional over $X$. The semipositive metric it supplies on $-K_Y+A_0$ has locally integrable squared frame norms and is smooth and strictly positive on an ordinary open set. The correction-ring theorem of *Integrable metrics and effectivity with controlled boundary* (OpenAI 2026b, Theorem <span class="upright">(Finite generation near a correction divisor)</span>) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[CompanionE](#ref-CompanionE)] then gives finite generation in the two rational divisor directions selected by the input sequence.

The remaining argument uses the actual rational functions representing sections. In a finitely generated bigraded ring, a monomial whose normalized degree approaches a boundary ray uses off-ray generators in negligible degree. For each input section, the valuation inequality selects a monomial with no larger valuation. One boundary-ray generator therefore has sufficiently small normalized valuation. Its divisor is effective up to the correction $A_0$. Birational pushforward removes that correction; centeredness then turns the resulting upper bound on the valuation into exact equality with zero. This order of operations allows the exceptional correction itself to have positive valuation.

The endpoint argument belongs to the study of asymptotic vanishing and multigraded section rings. Ein, Lazarsfeld, Mustaţă, Nakamaye and Popa explain how finite generation controls asymptotic orders and base loci (Ein et al. 2006, sec. 4) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[ELMNP2006](#ref-ELMNP2006)], with the corrected polyhedral argument (Ein et al. 2023, Proposition 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[ELMNP2023Erratum](#ref-ELMNP2023Erratum)]; Corti and Lazić give a multigraded formulation (Corti and Lazić 2013, Theorem 3.5) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[CortiLazic2013](#ref-CortiLazic2013)]. Lemma <a href="#lem:endpoint" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:endpoint">3</a> gives the direct one-ray, one-valuation argument used here. The geometric finite-generation input rests on the adjoint-ring theory of Birkar, Cascini, Hacon and McKernan (Birkar et al. 2010) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[BCHM2010](#ref-BCHM2010)] and Cascini–Lazić (Cascini and Lazić 2012) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[CasciniLazic2012](#ref-CasciniLazic2012)], in the form (Corti and Lazić 2013, Theorem 3.2(1)) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[CortiLazic2013](#ref-CortiLazic2013)].

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### From orders to invariant sections

For a torus action, the differential of the action gives $-K_X$ its natural linearization. A nonzero section can transform by a nontrivial character, so scalar nonvanishing does not by itself give invariance. Along the generic orbit of a one-parameter subgroup, an eigensection of weight $w$ has order $\mu-w$, where $\mu$ is the fiber weight at the limit. Exact order zero therefore attains that fiber weight.

Müller’s equivariant nonvanishing theorem gives invariant anti-log-canonical sections for projective sub-log-canonical pairs with semiample anti-log-canonical divisor, under commutative linear algebraic group actions (Müller 2025, Theorem C) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Muller2025](#ref-Muller2025)]. For smooth varieties with a torus action, we obtain the following result under smooth semipositivity and a cohomological hypothesis.

<a id="stmt-v-invariant-main"></a>
<div id="V:invariant-main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2**. *Let $X$ be smooth connected projective over $\mathbb C$, with $-K_X$ smoothly semipositive and $H^i(X,\mathcal O_X)=0$ for every $i>0$. For every algebraic torus $T$ acting on $X$, some positive anticanonical multiple has a nonzero $T$-invariant section for its natural linearization.*

</div>

The proof starts with the source component, which attracts the generic orbit as the one-parameter subgroup tends to zero. The attracting decomposition of Białynicki-Birula identifies this component (Białynicki-Birula 1973) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[BB1973](#ref-BB1973)]. The holomorphic fixed-point formula of Atiyah–Bott, Atiyah–Segal and Atiyah–Singer (Atiyah and Bott 1968; Atiyah and Segal 1968; Atiyah and Singer 1968) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[AtiyahBott1968](#ref-AtiyahBott1968), [AtiyahSegal1968](#ref-AtiyahSegal1968), [AtiyahSinger1968](#ref-AtiyahSinger1968)] supplies cohomology classes of the corresponding extremal weight. Semipositive hard Lefschetz (Demailly, Peternell, and Schneider 2001, Theorem 0.1) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[DPS2001](#ref-DPS2001)] turns them into twisted differential forms. Saturated determinants, following the method of Lazić–Peternell (Lazić and Peternell 2018, Lemma 4.1) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[LP2018](#ref-LP2018)] and its anticanonical adaptation (Lazić et al. 2023, Lemma 5.1) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[LMPTX2023](#ref-LMPTX2023)], give effective divisors with bounded order along the generic orbit. Their character calculation retains the determinant-rank shift. Theorem <a href="#V:exact" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="V:exact">1</a> then supplies actual normalized section weights whose convex hull contains zero. A rational convex combination becomes an invariant product after clearing denominators. The comparison of fixed-fiber and section-weight polytopes in the semiample setting is developed in (Müller 2025, Proposition 3.13 and Corollary 3.14) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Muller2025](#ref-Muller2025)]; here exact attainment supplies the supporting section weights.

Together with the structural inputs described below, the invariant sections yield the following scalar consequence: for every smooth connected projective complex variety $X$ with smoothly semipositive $-K_X$, there is an integer $m>0$ such that $$H^0(X,-mK_X)\ne0.$$ Sections <a href="#V:isometric-descent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="V:isometric-descent">13</a> and <a href="#V:sec-mrc-descent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="V:sec-mrc-descent">14</a> give two descents. The first uses the compact-monodromy structure theorem of *Anticanonical nonvanishing from smooth semipositivity* (OpenAI 2026a, Theorem <span class="upright">(Finite cover with compact torus monodromy)</span>) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[CompanionH](#ref-CompanionH)], based on the universal-cover structure of Demailly–Peternell–Schneider and its rational-connectedness refinement by Campana–Demailly–Peternell (Demailly, Peternell, and Schneider 1996; Campana, Demailly, and Peternell 2015) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[DPS1996](#ref-DPS1996), [CDP2015](#ref-CDP2015)]. The second uses a locally constant maximal rationally connected fibration, supplied by Matsumura–Wang (Matsumura and Wang 2023) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[MatsumuraWangV3](#ref-MatsumuraWangV3)], following the earlier smooth structure theorem of Cao–Höring (Cao and Höring 2019) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[CaoHoring](#ref-CaoHoring)]. It follows Müller’s invariant-section reduction (Müller 2025, Theorem 2.2) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Muller2025](#ref-Muller2025)] and includes the unipotent part of the commutative algebraic monodromy. The compact-monodromy argument uses only the structural and finite-cover norm results of the nonvanishing companion; its nonvanishing theorem is not an input.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Independent methods and their additional outputs

The exact-order theorem and its invariant application are followed by two independent constructions. Their purpose is to retain different information during the passage from twisted sections to anticanonical sections.

The first uses nef reduction and extension. Given bounded orders in $|m_jL+M|$, with $q(X)=0$ and $-M$ pseudoeffective, it separates a fixed divisor of zero arc order from a line bundle on a base. A fiber-maximum metric makes that line nef; the nef reduction of Bauer and collaborators (Bauer et al. 2002) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[BCEKPRSW2002](#ref-BCEKPRSW2002)] removes its numerical null directions. Extension of first jets by the theorem of Cao–Demailly–Matsumura (Cao, Demailly, and Matsumura 2017) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[CDM2017](#ref-CDM2017)] proves that the remaining base line is big and yields arbitrarily small normalized arc orders. Theorem <a href="#V:approximate-arc" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="V:approximate-arc">14</a> records this approximate transfer, which the exact theorem already strengthens under the same hypotheses. The independent proof also gives the bigness and jet construction. Its character application, Proposition <a href="#V:extremal-invariants" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="V:extremal-invariants">15</a>, uses $q(X)=0$ and $\chi(X,\mathcal O_X)\ne0$ instead of vanishing of all higher structure-sheaf cohomology, together with the stated equivariant very ample linearization.

There is a second ending to this nef-reduction construction. Starting with invariant sections of $jL-G$ for unbounded $j$, where $G$ is a fixed pseudoeffective linearized line bundle, it forms a base from invariant rational functions with pseudoeffectively controlled poles. It then extends one nonzero invariant class in a multiplier-ideal quotient. Theorem <a href="#V:invariant-conversion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="V:invariant-conversion">16</a> thus removes the fixed error while keeping invariance throughout. This ending needs $q(X)=0$ but does not require the first-jet or weight-polytope steps. The common tools occupy Section <a href="#V:sec-nef" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="V:sec-nef">8</a>, the arc and character applications Sections <a href="#V:sec-transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="V:sec-transfer">9</a> and <a href="#V:sec-extremal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="V:sec-extremal">10</a>, and the invariant conversion Section <a href="#V:sec-invariant-field" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="V:sec-invariant-field">11</a>.

The other finite-generation construction assumes rational connectedness and controls an entire collection of divisor classes. A prime divisor $D$ is controlled when $aL-D$ is pseudoeffective for some positive integer $a$. Proposition <a href="#V:controlled-cone" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="V:controlled-cone">19</a> places the naturally linearized classes of all invariant controlled primes in one rational polyhedral cone generated by invariant effective rational divisors. Its generators need not themselves be controlled. Here the effectivity companion’s weighted-approximation theorem produces an adjoint boundary on a base; its correction disappears under birational pushforward. Finite generation of the full multigraded ring and then of its invariant subring gives the cone. Character separation supplies another proof of invariant nonvanishing in the rationally connected case, as well as the fiberwise input used in the locally constant MRC descent. Section <a href="#V:controlled-cone-section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="V:controlled-cone-section">12</a> keeps this weighted metric construction distinct from the unweighted integrability used for exact attainment.

The proof of exact attainment is organized as follows. Section <a href="#sec:generator" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:generator">2</a> proves the finite-generator lemma and its birational application. Sections <a href="#V:null-field" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="V:null-field">3</a> and <a href="#V:exact-proof" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="V:exact-proof">4</a> construct the null-polar base and prove Theorem <a href="#V:exact" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="V:exact">1</a>; Section <a href="#sec:arc" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:arc">5</a> gives the formal-arc consequence. Section <a href="#V:sec-order-weight" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="V:sec-order-weight">6</a> establishes the order/weight identity, and Section <a href="#subsec:valuation-extremal-weights" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="subsec:valuation-extremal-weights">7</a> proves Theorem <a href="#V:invariant-main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="V:invariant-main">2</a>. The independent methods then supply the additional conclusions just described, before the paired monodromy descents finish the scalar nonvanishing consequence.

<a id="sec:generator"></a>
### Selecting a generator on the boundary ray

For a field $K$ containing $\mathbb C$, a real-valued valuation is a map $v:K^\times\to\mathbb R$ satisfying $$v(fg)=v(f)+v(g),\qquad
v(f+g)\geq\min\{v(f),v(g)\}\quad(f+g\ne0).$$ All valuations in this section are trivial on $\mathbb C^\times$. The zero valuation is allowed. If a homogeneous polynomial has the form $f x^a y^b$, we apply $v$ to its coefficient $f$; the formal variables $x,y$ record degrees only.

The relevant boundary ray consists of degrees $(0,b)$. A sequence approaches that ray after normalization when its first degree is negligible compared with its total degree.

<a id="stmt-lem-endpoint"></a>
<div id="lem:endpoint" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3**. *Let $R\subset K[x,y]$ be a finitely generated $\mathbb C$-subalgebra, graded by $\mathbb N^2$ with $\deg x=(1,0)$, $\deg y=(0,1)$, and $R_{0,0}=\mathbb C$. Let $v$ be a real-valued valuation of $K$, trivial on $\mathbb C^\times$. Suppose that nonzero homogeneous elements $$s_j=f_jx^{a_j}y^{b_j}\in R,\qquad d_j=a_j+b_j>0,$$ satisfy $$\frac{a_j}{d_j}\longrightarrow0,
\qquad
\frac{v(f_j)}{d_j}\longrightarrow\gamma\in\mathbb R.$$ Then every finite set of nonzero homogeneous generators of $R$ contains a generator $f x^0y^b$, with $b>0$, such that $$\frac{v(f)}b\leq\gamma.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-endpoint"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-endpoint</summary>

<a id="eq:monomial"></a>
*Proof.* Discard constant generators and write the others as $$r_i=g_ix^{u_i}y^{w_i},
\qquad u_i,w_i\in\mathbb N,\qquad u_i+w_i>0.$$ There are finitely many indices $i$. Because the generators are homogeneous, $s_j$ can be expressed as a sum of monomials of degree $(a_j,b_j)$. One nonzero monomial has coefficient valuation at most $v(f_j)$: otherwise the valuation inequality for the sum would contradict its coefficient $f_j$. Let $e_{ij}$ be the exponents of the generators in such a monomial. Nonzero scalar coefficients have valuation zero, so $$\label{eq:monomial}
\sum_i e_{ij}u_i=a_j,\qquad
\sum_i e_{ij}w_i=b_j,\qquad
\sum_i e_{ij}v(g_i)\leq v(f_j).$$

We compare the factors with $u_i>0$ and those with $u_i=0$. Every positive $u_i$ is at least one; hence $$\sum_{u_i>0}e_{ij}\leq a_j.$$ Since the generating set is finite, there are constants $C_1,C_2\geq0$ such that these factors contribute at most $C_1a_j$ to the second degree and at least $-C_2a_j$ to the valuation. For example, take $$C_1=\max_i w_i,\qquad
C_2=\max_i\max\{0,-v(g_i)\}.$$ The set of nonconstant generators is nonempty because $d_j>0$.

Some generator must have $u_i=0$. Otherwise <a href="#eq:monomial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:monomial">[eq:monomial]</a> would give $b_j\leq C_1a_j$, contrary to $a_j/d_j\to0$. For every such generator $w_i>0$, so we may define $$\lambda=\min_{u_i=0}\frac{v(g_i)}{w_i}.$$ Let $c_j$ be the second degree contributed by the factors with $u_i>0$. Thus $0\leq c_j\leq C_1a_j$. The remaining factors contribute second degree $b_j-c_j$, and their valuation is at least $\lambda(b_j-c_j)$. Equation <a href="#eq:monomial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:monomial">[eq:monomial]</a> therefore gives $$v(f_j)\geq
\lambda b_j-\bigl(C_2+|\lambda|C_1\bigr)a_j.$$ Divide by $d_j$ and pass to the limit. Since $b_j/d_j\to1$, we obtain $\gamma\geq\lambda$. A generator realizing the finite minimum $\lambda$ proves the assertion. ◻

</details>

The proof needs the finite list of actual coefficients $g_i$. It does not follow from knowing only a limiting cone of numerical classes. This is the feature that will matter for divisor section rings in the next subsection.

<a id="sec:geometric"></a>
#### Removing a birational correction

We now specify the geometric data to which the lemma applies. The main point is that the correction on the base need not have valuation zero. It will disappear under birational pushforward before the last valuation comparison.

All varieties below are integral projective varieties over $\mathbb C$. For a Cartier divisor $B$ on a normal variety $Y$, we identify sections with rational functions: $$H^0(Y,\mathcal O_Y(B))
=\{h\in\mathbb C(Y)^\times:\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_Y(h)+B\geq0\}\cup\{0\}.$$ For two Cartier divisors $B_1,B_0$, their bigraded section ring is <a id="eq:section-ring"></a>$$\label{eq:section-ring}
R(Y;B_1,B_0)
=\bigoplus_{a,b\in\mathbb N}
H^0\bigl(Y,\mathcal O_Y(aB_1+bB_0)\bigr)x^ay^b
\subset\mathbb C(Y)[x,y].$$ Projectivity and integrality give $H^0(Y,\mathcal O_Y)=\mathbb C$, as required in Lemma <a href="#lem:endpoint" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:endpoint">3</a>.

We also use rational powers of rational functions, interpreted as elements of $\mathbb C(Y)^\times\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb Q$, written multiplicatively. The divisor map and every valuation extend $\mathbb Q$-linearly to this group. Each element becomes an ordinary rational function after a positive integral power is taken. No extension of the function field by actual roots is intended.

A valuation of $\mathbb C(X)$ is centered on $X$ if its valuation ring dominates $\mathcal O_{X,\xi}$ for some point $\xi\in X$. For a Cartier divisor $D$, define $v(D)$ by applying $v$ to a local defining rational function at $\xi$. This is independent of the equation, because units have valuation zero, and extends $\mathbb Q$-linearly to rational Cartier divisors. In particular, $v(D)\geq0$ for every effective rational Cartier divisor $D$. The zero valuation is centered at the generic point.

<a id="stmt-prop-geometric"></a>
<div id="prop:geometric" class="proposition">

**Proposition 4**. *Let $X,Y$ be smooth integral projective complex varieties, let $W$ be normal and projective, and let $$X\xleftarrow{\ p\ }W\xrightarrow{\ f\ }Y$$ be morphisms, with $p$ birational and $f$ surjective and equidimensional. Identify $\mathbb C(Y)$ with its induced subfield of $\mathbb C(X)=\mathbb C(W)$. Fix a centered discrete valuation $v$ of $\mathbb C(X)$, allowing also the zero valuation.*

<a id="eq:minimum-divisor"></a>
*Let $A\geq0$ be a rational divisor on $Y$ such that $p_*f^*A=0$. Let $D_0,D_1$ be rational Cartier divisors on $X$ and put $$D(t)=(1-t)D_0+tD_1\qquad(0\leq t\leq1).$$ For each prime divisor $Q$ of $Y$, define $$\label{eq:minimum-divisor}
P(t)=\sum_Q
\min_{E:\,f(E)=Q}
\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{coeff}}_E p^*D(t)}{\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E(f^*Q)}\,Q,$$ where $E$ ranges over the prime divisors of $W$ dominating $Q$. Assume that $P(t)=(1-t)P(0)+tP(1)$ throughout $[0,1]$.*

*Suppose that rational numbers $t_j\in[0,1]$ tend to zero and that $h_j\in\mathbb C(Y)^\times\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb Q$ satisfy $$N_j=D(t_j)+\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_X(h_j)\geq0,\qquad v(N_j)\longrightarrow0.$$ Finally, suppose that for some $\epsilon\in\mathbb Q_{>0}$ and integer $k>0$, the divisors $$B_1=k\bigl(A+\epsilon P(1)\bigr),\qquad
B_0=k\bigl(A+\epsilon P(0)\bigr)$$ are Cartier and the ring $R(Y;B_1,B_0)$ is finitely generated. Then there is an effective rational divisor $$N\sim_{\mathbb Q}D_0,\qquad v(N)=0.$$*

</div>

The minimum in <a href="#eq:minimum-divisor" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:minimum-divisor">[eq:minimum-divisor]</a> is over a finite nonempty set, since $f$ is surjective and equidimensional. Only finitely many base primes occur: the pullbacks of $D_0,D_1$ have fixed finite support. Each coefficient is a minimum of finitely many rational affine functions, so $P(t)$ is piecewise affine. In applications one restricts to a sufficiently small rational segment ending at $t=0$ and rescales the parameter to obtain the stated affine hypothesis. If $Y$ is a point, the sum is empty and means $P(t)=0$.

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-geometric"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-geometric</summary>

*Proof.* Write $$t_j=\frac{a_j}{d_j},\qquad d_j=a_j+b_j>0,
\qquad a_j,b_j\in\mathbb N.$$ Scale the pair $(a_j,b_j)$ by a positive integer whenever necessary to clear denominators. The ratio $a_j/d_j=t_j$ is unchanged. In particular we can arrange that $$s_j=h_j^{\epsilon k d_j}$$ is an ordinary rational function on $Y$.

<a id="eq:base-effectivity"></a>
The effective divisor $N_j$ is rational Cartier because $X$ is smooth, so $p^*N_j\geq0$. For a prime $E$ of $W$ dominating a base prime $Q$, let $e_E=\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_E(f^*Q)>0$. Taking its coefficient in this pullback gives $$\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{coeff}}_E p^*D(t_j)}{e_E}
+\mathop{\mathrm{coeff}}_Q\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_Y(h_j)\geq0.$$ Taking the minimum proves $$\label{eq:base-effectivity}
P(t_j)+\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_Y(h_j)\geq0.$$ The affine hypothesis now gives $$a_jB_1+b_jB_0=k d_j\bigl(A+\epsilon P(t_j)\bigr).$$ Since $A\geq0$, equation <a href="#eq:base-effectivity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:base-effectivity">[eq:base-effectivity]</a> shows that $s_jx^{a_j}y^{b_j}$ is a nonzero homogeneous element of $R(Y;B_1,B_0)$.

<a id="eq:endpoint-effectivity"></a>
The valuation of $D(t)$ is affine in $t$, since the two divisors have fixed support and valuation is $\mathbb Q$-linear. Thus $$v(h_j)=v(N_j)-v(D(t_j))\longrightarrow-v(D_0),
\qquad
\frac{v(s_j)}{d_j}\longrightarrow-\epsilon k\,v(D_0).$$ Apply Lemma <a href="#lem:endpoint" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:endpoint">3</a> to this section ring and to the restriction of $v$ to $\mathbb C(Y)$. The lemma only uses this restriction as a real-valued valuation. We obtain a generator represented by a rational function $r$, of degree $(0,b)$ with $b>0$, such that $$\frac{v(r)}{\epsilon k b}\leq-v(D_0).$$ Put $h_*=r^{1/(\epsilon k b)}$ in the rational-power group. The section inequality for $r$ gives $$\label{eq:endpoint-effectivity}
P(0)+A/\epsilon+\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_Y(h_*)\geq0.$$

We have selected the endpoint section on $Y$. It remains to show that it gives an effective divisor on $X$ without retaining the correction $A/\epsilon$.

A prime of $W$ is horizontal if it dominates $Y$, and vertical otherwise. Principal divisors pulled back from $Y$ have no horizontal components. Consequently the horizontal coefficients of $p^*D(t_j)$ are nonnegative, by effectivity of $p^*N_j$; the same is true for $p^*D_0$ by passage to the limit. Every vertical prime dominates a base prime, by equidimensionality. For such a prime $E$ over $Q$, the minimum defining $P(0)$ and <a href="#eq:endpoint-effectivity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:endpoint-effectivity">[eq:endpoint-effectivity]</a> show that its coefficient in $$p^*D_0+
f^*\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_Y(h_*)+A/\epsilon\bigr)$$ is nonnegative. This divisor is therefore effective.

Push it forward by $p$. The assumption $p_*f^*A=0$ removes the correction, while pullback and pushforward of the principal divisor give $\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_X(h_*)$. Hence $$N=D_0+\mathop{\mathrm{div}}_X(h_*)\geq0,\qquad N\sim_{\mathbb Q}D_0.$$ Centering gives $v(N)\geq0$. The selected generator gives $v(N)=v(D_0)+v(h_*)\leq0$, proving equality. ◻

</details>

The proof never evaluates the correction $f^*A$ at $v$. Its removal is a divisor identity under pushforward. This permits an exceptional correction with positive valuation on a higher model.

<a id="V:null-field"></a>
### The field selected by the curvature intersection

We now construct the base needed for Theorem <a href="#V:exact" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="V:exact">1</a>. The data $X,L,\alpha,\nu,G_0,\ell$ remain as in the introduction. A divisor is called *null* in this section if it is effective and has $\ell$-value zero. Nonnegativity of $\ell$ implies that every effective divisor bounded by a null divisor is again null.

<a id="stmt-v-polar-field"></a>
<div id="V:polar-field" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5**. *The set $$k=\{0\}\cup\{h\in\mathbb C(X)^\times:\ell((h)_\infty)=0\}$$ is a finitely generated subfield of $\mathbb C(X)$, relatively algebraically closed in $\mathbb C(X)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-v-polar-field"><summary>Proof of stmt-v-polar-field</summary>

*Proof.* The polar divisors of $h_1+h_2$ and $h_1h_2$ are bounded by the sum of the polar divisors of $h_1,h_2$. The zero and polar divisors of a nonzero rational function are linearly equivalent, and therefore have the same $\ell$-value. These observations prove closure under addition, multiplication and inversion.

Suppose that $b\in\mathbb C(X)$ is algebraic over $k$. In a monic equation $b^r+a_1b^{r-1}+\cdots+a_r=0$, each coefficient belongs to $k$. At a prime divisor $P$ where $b$ has a pole, the term $b^r$ cannot have strictly smaller order than every other term. Thus, for some $i$, $i\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_P(b)\ge\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_P(a_i)$. In particular the pole order of $b$ is bounded by the sum of the pole orders of the coefficients. Its polar divisor is null, so $b\in k$.

Choose a transcendence basis $t_1,\ldots,t_d$ of $k/\mathbb C$. The algebraic closure of $\mathbb C(t_1,\ldots,t_d)$ inside the finitely generated field $\mathbb C(X)$ is a finite extension of that rational function field. The intermediate field $k$ is algebraic over the same field and is therefore finite over it. This proves finite generation. ◻

</details>

Choose smooth projective models and morphisms $$X\xleftarrow{\ \pi\ } Z\xrightarrow{\ g\ }Y,
 \qquad \mathbb C(Y)=k,$$ with $\pi$ birational and $g$ surjective. Characteristic zero and relative algebraic closedness imply that its generic fiber is geometrically integral. We first verify two properties which persist under every birational change of these models.

<a id="stmt-v-null-rank"></a>
<div id="V:null-rank" class="lemma">

<a id="V:ample-null"></a>
<a id="V:zero-rank"></a>
**Lemma 6**. *For every very ample divisor $H$ on $Y$, $$\label{V:ample-null}
 \ell(\pi_*g^*H)=0.$$ If $E_Z=K_{Z/X}$ is the effective Jacobian divisor, then $$\label{V:zero-rank}
 \dim_{\mathbb C(Y)}H^0(Z_\eta,\mathcal O_Z(E_Z)|_{Z_\eta})=1.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-v-null-rank"><summary>Proof of stmt-v-null-rank</summary>

*Proof.* Choose a basis $s_0,\ldots,s_N$ of the very ample system. The functions $s_i/s_0$ belong to $k$, so their polar divisors on $X$ are bounded by one null divisor $D$. Their poles on $Z$ are then bounded by $\pi^*D$. Base point freeness says that the componentwise maximum of those polar divisors on $Z$ is $g^*\mathop{\mathrm{div}}(s_0)$. After pushforward, effectivity and monotonicity give $0\le\ell(\pi_*g^*H)\le\ell(D)=0$.

The Jacobian section $s_{E_Z}$ gives one nonzero section in <a href="#V:zero-rank" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="V:zero-rank">[V:zero-rank]</a>. Divide any other generic-fiber section by it. The resulting rational function $a\in\mathbb C(Z)=\mathbb C(X)$ has horizontal poles bounded by $E_Z$. There are finitely many remaining polar primes. Each maps into a proper closed subset of $Y$. A sufficiently high very ample system has an effective member $B$ vanishing to prescribed orders on their images; increasing these orders ensures $$(a)_\infty\le E_Z+g^*B.$$ This also works when a vertical prime maps to codimension greater than one. Since $E_Z$ is exceptional over $X$, pushforward and <a href="#V:ample-null" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="V:ample-null">[V:ample-null]</a> show that the polar divisor of $a$ on $X$ is null. Thus $a\in k$. Every generic section is a scalar multiple of the Jacobian section, as claimed. ◻

</details>

The equality in <a href="#V:zero-rank" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="V:zero-rank">[V:zero-rank]</a> concerns the full adjoint space in degree zero. It has not been deduced from an eventual rank statement. It identifies the integrated Jacobian volume with a line metric on the base, rather than with a selected subspace of a larger direct image.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### An equidimensional model without a field extension

<a id="V:model"></a>
Assume $d=\dim Y>0$. We need a normal projective variety $W$ and a diagram $$\label{V:model}
 X\xleftarrow{\ p\ }W\xrightarrow{\ f\ }Y$$ with $p$ birational, $Y$ smooth projective, and $f$ equidimensional and toroidal for strict toroidal embeddings. All modifications here are birational; in particular the field of the base remains $k$. A toroidal embedding is locally modeled on a toric variety with its dense torus; the complement is its boundary. Strictness means that the irreducible boundary components are normal. Toroidal morphisms are locally toric morphisms, so boundary strata determine rational cones and the morphism determines their lattice-linear maps.

Weak toroidalization (Abramovich, Denef, and Karu 2013, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[ADK2013](#ref-ADK2013)] first gives a birational toroidal diagram with smooth projective target. For a toroidal morphism, equidimensionality is equivalent to the condition that every source cone maps onto a target cone (Abramovich and Karu 2000, Lemma 4.1 and Remark 4.2) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[AK2000](#ref-AK2000)]. Refine the target cone complex so that the image of every source cone is a union of cones. Projective refinements exist by (Abramovich and Karu 2000, Lemma 4.3) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[AK2000](#ref-AK2000)]; further projective toroidal resolution makes the target nonsingular. Intersecting the source cones with inverse images of target cones gives projective source subdivisions and a compatible toroidal morphism. In a target cone of the new subdivision, an old cone image meets it in a union of faces which is convex, hence in a face. Thus the new source cones map onto target cones. This is the equidimensionalization of (Abramovich and Karu 2000, Proposition 4.4) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[AK2000](#ref-AK2000)].

In local toric models, a source stratum maps submersively onto its target stratum. Cone surjectivity bounds the dimension of the fiber in each stratum above by $n-d$; the opposite bound for full fibers follows because the target is smooth. This gives equidimensionality. The normal source may remain singular, and no covering to reduce multiplicities is needed. Resolve it by $Z\to W$ when using smooth differential forms; that resolution need not itself be equidimensional. We continue to write $\pi:Z\to X$ and $g:Z\to Y$ for the induced maps.
